# Known limits and trade-offs

Every limit of Tracekit is written down next to the feature it belongs to, which makes it easy to miss when you read
only one page. This page collects them in one place. Each release's notes link here, and a doc that adds a "Limits"
section must be linked from this page (`tests/test_docs.py` checks it).

Each row says what the limit is, why it is so, what to do about it, and where the details are. Rows marked
**(1.1)** describe behaviour new in 1.1, not in 1.0; **(1.2)**, new in 1.2; **(1.3)**, new in 1.3; **(1.4)**, new in 1.4.

What Tracekit guarantees and doesn't, per deployment, on one page: [guarantees](guarantees.md).

## Evidence and verification

| Limit | Why it is so | What to do | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| A verified bundle doesn't prove intent. | A record says a call was made, not why. | Read decisions, approvals and reasons alongside the records. | [FAQ](faq.md#what-cant-tracekit-prove) |
| A verified bundle doesn't prove that everything the agent did was recorded. | An action that crosses no capture layer (a subprocess, code that skips the adapter, a call around a gateway that isn't mandatory) leaves no record. **(1.2)** What some layer saw and nothing reported is now a signed gap: a call allowed or approved that never completed (`decided_not_completed`), a tool result the gateway saw go back with no decide (`reconcile.hook_missing`, at once), client counter values never received (`client_counter_gap`, also at `close_run`), writes after `run.final` (`late_activity`), a second run from inside a bound harness process (`concurrent_run`), a tailer that stops (`tailer_lost`) and a second tailer token for a run (`tailer_redelegated`). A call no layer saw still leaves nothing. | Read the `coverage`, `tiers` and gap lines; add an out-of-process layer (the [LLM gateway](gateway.md), the [tool executor](executor.md), the [MCP gateway](mcp-gateway.md)). | [FAQ](faq.md#what-cant-tracekit-prove), [verdicts](verdicts.md#informational-lines-always-pass) |
| A verified bundle doesn't prove that a tool result the agent reported is true. | In-process capture (T1) records what the agent's process reported; only out-of-process capture (T2) sees what crossed a boundary. | Prefer T2 evidence for calls that matter; use `executor: t2` approvals. | [FAQ](faq.md#what-cant-tracekit-prove), [approvals](approvals.md#tiers-t1-and-t2-executors) |
| A `tool.result`'s `output.size` is the size of what the signer received. For a tool whose result the adapters send as its digest (every tool the policy doesn't list as untrusted), that is the digest's size, not the result's. | The signer records what it can check; a size the client claimed would be the agent's word. | Read `output.size` as the size of the content committed to; list a tool under `untrusted:` to have its result itself sent, redacted and committed to. | [privacy](privacy.md) |
| Imported OpenTelemetry (T3) proves only that spans were received. | Spans are exported after the work is done, by the application. | Gate calls with an adapter or hook; treat T3 runs as a log, not evidence of completeness. | [OpenTelemetry](otel.md#receiving-traces-tier-t3-import) |
| agentgateway's MCP spans import without arguments or results, and each request is its own trace, so its own run, unless the agent sends `traceparent`. | agentgateway's spans name the tool and its outcome; the import keys runs by trace id. | Add `gen_ai.tool.call.arguments` and `gen_ai.tool.call.result` through agentgateway's tracing `attributes`; send `traceparent` from the agent; for evidence of what ran, use the `ext_mcp` processor. | [agentgateway](integrations/agentgateway.md#the-otlp-import) |
| An imported run closes when its trace's root span arrives; a trace whose root never reaches the signer (its root is another process's, as with `traceparent` through agentgateway) stays open until `idle_s` and counts against the importer's `open_runs` (100): past it every batch is refused (`503`, retried) until runs idle out. A span that ends after its root and comes more than `grace_s` behind it is rejected. | The import can't tell a trace is done but by its root; open runs are held in memory. | Export the root span to the signer too, raise the importer's `open_runs` in `limits`, or lower `idle_s`. | [OpenTelemetry](otel.md#receiving-traces-tier-t3-import) |
| Tracekit is tamper-evident, not tamper-proof: a deletion shows, it isn't prevented. | Prevention would need attested hardware (a TEE), which Tracekit doesn't claim. | Keep independent witnesses and a monitor so a rewrite is detected. | [FAQ](faq.md#what-cant-tracekit-prove) |
| Whoever holds the signer's key can rewrite or fork its log; nothing in the bundle alone shows it. | Signatures prove who signed, not that the key holder didn't sign again. | Pin a witness the operator doesn't run (`witnessed`) and a monitor (`witnessed+monitored`). | [server threat model](threat-model-server.md#s4-the-operator-organisation-and-the-exporter) |
| Records after the last cosigned checkpoint are unproven. | A witness vouches only for what it has cosigned. | Export after the next checkpoint; read the `log tail` warning. | [verdicts](verdicts.md#warnings---strict-exits-3), [witnesses](witnesses.md#trust-model-per-witness-type) |
| A bundle exported before its run is finalised verifies only `TO HEAD n (open)`. | The signer finalises a run a few seconds after it closes. | Re-export after `run.final`; for audits require `VERIFIED`. | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#export-and-verify-by-hand), [verdicts](verdicts.md#integrity) |
| **(1.4)** A run cut back (records or parts dropped from its end, with an inclusion proof of the record now last) verifies `TO HEAD n (open)`, as an export before its end does: nothing shows that records were dropped. | The proofs file isn't signed, and from a run's records an inclusion proof of any of them can often be computed; only the signer's `run.final` or a run-set says where a run ends. | For audits require `VERIFIED` (the run with its `run.final`), or a run-set with `run-set: COMPLETE`. | [format v2](format-v2.md#9-bundle-layout), [verdicts](verdicts.md#integrity) |
| A single run's bundle doesn't show that no other run was left out. | The exporter chooses which runs to export. | Ask for the tenant's run-set bundle and require `run-set: COMPLETE`. | [auditor guide](auditor-guide.md#3-get-the-bundles) |
| **(1.4)** `tracekit export --v2 --run-set` always starts at registry size 0 and bundles every finished run of the tenant, one file each: past about 10,000 finished runs (the format's 10,000 files) or 512 MiB in all it refuses, saying which limit it is over. | The command has no later start, and the format caps a bundle's files and size so that verifying it needs bounded memory. | Give each workload or team its own tenant, or start a new `data_dir` (a new log) before a tenant nears the limit. | [auditor guide](auditor-guide.md#3-get-the-bundles) |
| A withheld key retirement isn't visible unless a run-set from registry size 0 reaches the bundle. A bundle whose key records show one key and no retirement says so only on its `Assurance` line, not as a warning: a retirement withheld together with its new key would look the same. | Only the registry log proves which `key.retire` records exist. | Read `key retirements not proven complete` on the `Assurance` line (a `keys` warning when the key records show a retired key); require a run-set from registry size 0 where it matters. | [verdicts](verdicts.md#warnings---strict-exits-3) |
| `key assurance` is `asserted` unless a record-key issuer certifies the keys. | Without an issuer the log declares its own record keys. | Run an [issuer](issuer.md) and pin it in the trust config. | [issuer](issuer.md#verifying) |
| The verifier doesn't fetch revocations. | Verification is offline and takes trust only from the verifier's side. | Pass the issuer's log with `--revocations`. | [issuer](issuer.md#limits) |
| A trust config the operator supplies proves nothing about the operator. | The bundle carries no code or trust (I9); whoever writes the trust config decides what verifies. | Build `trust.json` from independent sources and install the verifier yourself. | [auditor guide](auditor-guide.md#2-build-the-trust-config-from-independent-sources) |
| A v1 bundle verified without `--key` or `--witness` is only internally consistent (`UNANCHORED`). | A bundle carries its own public key, so a bundle forged with a fresh key is consistent too. | Pin the signer's key or an independent witness. | [laptop threat model](threat-model-laptop.md#trust-roots-for-verification) |
| v1: the exporter can replace a selected record with an elided stub, and `manifest.json` is unsigned. | v1 stubs are not bound to a run; the manifest is an index, recomputed from signed records. | Use v2 bundles; read the "run completeness unproven" warning. | [laptop threat model](threat-model-laptop.md#open-problems) |
| Record timestamps come from the signer host's clock; `seq` and the chain prove order only. | Only witnesses and RFC 3161 timestamps give independent time. | Read cosignature and anchor times in the `Assurance` line. | [laptop threat model](threat-model-laptop.md#what-each-attack-comes-to), [witnesses](witnesses.md#v2-rekor-v2-anchors-and-rfc-3161-timestamps) |
| A v1 record after a v1 → v2 bridge is reported only with `--v1-ledger`. | The frozen v1 verifier sees bridge records as ordinary gaps. | Verify bridged logs with `--v1-ledger`. | [format v2](format-v2.md#12-v1--v2-bridge) |
| The TypeScript verifier reports `UNVERIFIABLE` for bundles that rely on a Rekor anchor, an SLH-DSA checkpoint signature or certified record keys. | Web Crypto has no SLH-DSA, and those checks aren't ported yet; it never says `VERIFIED` for a check it skipped. | Verify such bundles with the Python verifier (`pip install tracekit-ai`). | [auditor guide](auditor-guide.md), [format v2](format-v2.md#13-implementations) |
| A verifier doesn't check what an ext name it doesn't interpret says: the records verify (signature, chain, inclusion), and each such name is a `not interpreted` warning. | Format 1.4's non-critical slot lets a later Tracekit add what an older verifier can leave unread, instead of locking that verifier out. | Verify with the version the warning names (`tracekit >= x checks it`); `--strict` stops on it. | [format v2](format-v2.md#9-bundle-layout), [verdicts](verdicts.md#warnings---strict-exits-3) |
| A 1.3 or older verifier reports a bundle with an `ext` field or record `UNVERIFIABLE (needs tracekit >= 1.4.0)` and prints no commands to verify it. | The slot and the upgrade lines are new in 1.4.0. | Run the commands in [verdicts](verdicts.md#a-bundle-that-needs-a-newer-verifier). | [format v2](format-v2.md#9-bundle-layout) |
| The browser verifier (`verify.html`) is the TypeScript verifier: it reports `UNVERIFIABLE` where that one does (SLH-DSA, Rekor anchors, certified record keys, `tracekit why` findings) and takes no monitor reports, revocations or v1 ledger; a v1 bundle it names the command for. Without a trust file it is never `VERIFIED`. In a browser that refuses its worker it verifies in the page itself, which a bundle of 100k records holds for seconds. A copy someone else hosts is only as good as its bytes. | It runs on Web Crypto in the page; a browser can't check where a page came from. | Use `tracekit verify` for those checks and for very large bundles in such a browser; get the trust file from the sender; for evidence, open a copy you checked with `gh attestation verify verify.html --repo Cygnux-Labs/Tracekit`. | [format v2](format-v2.md#13-implementations), [releasing](RELEASING.md#verifying-a-release) |
| The browser verifier holds the bundle, unzipped, and every record it parses in the tab's memory, about 8 times the bundle unzipped: a run's 100,000 records (50,000 tool calls: a 28 MB bundle, 130 MiB unzipped in three parts) take about 14 s in Chrome, and the same verifier as a CLI 1 GB ([E22](evaluation.md#e22-verifier-agreement-on-real-bundles)). A bundle near the format's 512 MiB, some 390,000 records, would need about 4 GB, more than a tab may hold: it can close the tab. Without a trust file the page reads every record, since no pinned key stops a bundle at its checkpoint. | It verifies offline, in the page, under the format's limits (512 MiB unzipped); a line shorter than a signed record is refused before any is parsed, so a small file can't unzip into millions of records. | Choose the sender's trust file: a bundle their signer didn't sign then fails at its checkpoint before a record is read. Verify very large bundles with `tracekit verify` or `npx @cygnuxlabs/tracekit verify`. | [format v2](format-v2.md#9-bundle-layout) |
| A run's records must fit one bundle: 512 MiB unzipped, about 390,000 records (some 190,000 tool calls with small arguments, fewer with larger ones). **(1.4)** A run past 64 MiB of records (about 24,000 tool calls) is written in parts of up to 64 MiB, which a 1.3 or older verifier can't read: it reports the bundle `UNVERIFIABLE (needs tracekit >= 1.4.0)`. `tracekit export` refuses a bundle of more than 512 MiB in all or 10,000 files, and says which limit it is over. Both command-line verifiers hold about 8 times the bundle unzipped in memory: 1 GB for 50,000 tool calls, some 4 GB at the limit. | The format caps each file of a bundle at 64 MiB and the whole at 512 MiB, so verifying one needs bounded memory; a bundle past them is `UNUSABLE BUNDLE` to every verifier. | Verify a bundle with parts with tracekit 1.4 or newer (Python, TypeScript or the page), on a machine with 4 GB free for the largest. Start a new run for each task or session; a run still open exports up to its head, so export a very long one before it reaches the limit. | [format v2](format-v2.md#9-bundle-layout) |
| The TypeScript verifier needs Node 20.12 or newer (or a current browser). | It uses JSON imports and `DecompressionStream("deflate-raw")`, which earlier Node 20 releases lack. | Upgrade Node, or use the Python verifier. | [TypeScript SDK](../sdk/typescript/README.md) |
| `npx @cygnuxlabs/tracekit verify` with `--monitor-report`, `--revocations` or the v1 bridge options runs the Python verifier, which must be installed. | Only the Python verifier has those checks. | `pip install tracekit-ai`, or verify without those options. | [auditor guide](auditor-guide.md) |
| **(1.2)** A bundle holding a run started by another run (`parent_run`, a sub-agent's run) needs tracekit 1.2 or newer; a 1.1 verifier reports it `UNVERIFIABLE (needs tracekit >= 1.2.0)`. | Every v2 record definition is closed, so an older verifier can't read the new fields; the manifest's `verifier_min_version` makes it say so instead of `FAILED`. | Verify with tracekit 1.2 or newer (Python or TypeScript). | [format v2](format-v2.md#9-bundle-layout) |
| **(1.2)** A bundle holding a 1.2 completeness gap (`decided_not_completed`, `late_activity`, `concurrent_run`, `tailer_redelegated`, a gap's `run_id`), a `run.child` record or a run.final with `provider_executed` needs tracekit 1.2 or newer; older verifiers report it `UNVERIFIABLE (needs tracekit >= 1.2.0)`. | The new kinds and fields are outside the 1.1 schema; the manifest's `verifier_min_version` says so. | Verify with tracekit 1.2 or newer. | [format v2](format-v2.md#9-bundle-layout) |
| Experimental surfaces may change between releases and get less hardening than the stable core. | Few people run them yet; the stable core is what is tested end to end and fixed first. | Build on the stable core; each experimental command or server says so on stderr when it starts. | [what's stable](support.md) |
| JSON nested deeper than 128 levels is refused: a bundle file is unusable, a record fails, and a tool call's arguments are denied (`TK-ARGS-INVALID`). | A fixed depth makes every verifier and the signer read the same documents the same way, whatever their parser's limits. | Flatten arguments deeper than that. | [format v2](format-v2.md#1-canonical-json) |

## Dev mode vs system mode

| Limit | Why it is so | What to do | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| **v1 tracekitd:** witness publishing (git push, HTTP) runs under the signer's lock, so an unreachable witness stalls appends for up to 15–60 s; a local user can hold the 64 connections by trickling bytes (the dev socket is world-writable); `runs.json` is rewritten on every append. | The v1 daemon is single-writer and laptop-sized. | Use the v2 signer for anything shared or busy; keep v1 witnesses reachable. | [laptop threat model](threat-model-laptop.md) |
| **v1 tracekitd:** a restart takes every run of the ledger as active again, so past the run cap it writes `run_state_evicted` gaps for long-finished runs, and a run that never sent `run.start` loses its owner. Parallel hooks of one session can record a false `trace.tamper` (transcript length read before the send). Client state files (`~/.tracekit-client/runs/`, Gemini call ids, transcript acks) are never pruned. | v1 state is rebuilt from the ledger and kept per laptop. | Read those gaps as restart artefacts; prune `~/.tracekit-client/runs/` by hand. | [laptop threat model](threat-model-laptop.md) |
| Same-user dev mode protects nothing against the agent itself. | The dev signer runs as your user, so an agent that runs commands as you can read its key, stop it, or rewrite and re-sign its store. Policy rules (`TK-D007`, `TK-D013`) are tripwires. | Use system mode, a sidecar or a central signer for anything you need to rely on; dev bundles say `isolation: same-user`. | [FAQ](faq.md#what-does-same-user-dev-mode-protect), [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#what-dev-assurance-means) |
| `Assurance` describes checkpoint cosigning, not isolation: a same-user signer with a witness verifies as `witnessed`. | The two are independent properties. | Read `isolation` as well as `Assurance`. | [verdicts](verdicts.md#assurance), [witnesses](witnesses.md#v2-c2sp-tlog-witnesses) |
| A system-mode signer without a witness verifies as `Assurance: dev`. | Nothing outside the host shows the log wasn't rolled back. | Add a witness on another host that the operator doesn't run. | [deployment](deploy.md#what-decides-a-bundles-assurance) |
| System mode doesn't protect against root on the machine. | Root can read the key and rewrite the store. | Witnesses on another host detect a rewrite of what they cosigned. | [deployment](deploy.md#laptop-system-mode) |
| In system mode the agent can remove the hook from its own `~/.claude/settings.json`, or act outside Claude Code, with no record and no gap. | The hook lives in the agent user's settings. | Use admin-managed settings (`init --managed`, v1) or an out-of-process record (gateway). | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#system-mode-linux-macos-experimental) |
| v1 system mode (init as root without `--v2`) has no uninstall: `tracekit uninstall` takes the hooks out of user and project settings only, not out of admin-managed settings (`--managed`), and leaves tracekitd's service, `/var/lib/tracekit`, `/opt/tracekit` and `/etc/tracekit/client.json`. | v1 system mode is the legacy path; `sudo /opt/tracekit/bin/python -I -m tracekit uninstall --v2` removes v2 system mode. | Remove Tracekit's hook groups from the managed settings file, stop and remove the tracekitd unit (or launchd plist), then those paths. | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#system-mode-linux-macos-experimental) |
| Without harness binding, any process of the agent's user can write into the agent's runs or start runs of its own. | The signer knows the caller by uid; run tokens are readable by that uid. | Register the harness (`--harness`, Linux). | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#system-mode-linux-macos-experimental) |
| System mode requires an agent user that is not root and not in a sudo, wheel, admin, docker or similar group. | Such a user could take over the signer. | `tracekit doctor` (`D-AGENT-PRIV`) checks it. | [doctor](doctor.md#v2-checks) |
| When the transcript tailer's ACL or sudo rule can't be set up, every run records a `tailer_lost` gap instead. | The tailer needs read access to the agent's transcripts. | Fix the setup init reports. | [deployment](deploy.md#laptop-system-mode) |

## Signer and capture

| Limit | Why it is so | What to do | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| A tool class set to fail `open` runs unrecorded while the signer is down. | It is the setting you choose when an outage must not stop the agent; the next call writes a signed gap for exactly the missed calls. | Keep `fail_modes` closed (the default); the report warns on every fail-open class. | [FAQ](faq.md#what-happens-when-the-signer-is-down) |
| While the signer is unreachable, the LangChain adapter, the Claude Code hook and the Hermes Agent plugin pick a call's fail mode by the class the shipped default policy gives the tool (`terminal` is `shell`, an unmapped tool `unknown`); the MCP and Browser Use adapters by their fixed class (`mcp`, `browser`); the OpenAI Agents, Claude Agent SDK and CrewAI adapters apply `default` to every tool. A signer whose policy maps the tool to another class may have another fail mode for it. | The tool's class is the signer's decision, and the signer can't be asked. | Keep fail modes closed for classes your policy remaps, or set `default`; with OpenAI Agents, the Claude Agent SDK or CrewAI, set `default`. | [FAQ](faq.md#what-happens-when-the-signer-is-down) |
| Model calls (autotrace) while the signer is down: under a closed `model` fail mode a request that can't be recorded is refused (`PermissionError`, saying so and what to do); a response the signer missed (it went down mid-call) is sent again at the next model call or at exit, after a signed `client_counter_gap` for the try that failed. A signer that stays down until the process exits leaves the request without its response, and the run open until the signer's idle timeout. | The client records only while the signer answers, and keeps missed responses in memory (the newest 1024). | Keep the signer running (system mode restarts it); set `fail_modes: {model: open}` to let model calls run unrecorded during an outage. | [FAQ](faq.md#what-happens-when-the-signer-is-down) |
| v1 hooks allow a call when the payload can't be parsed (Codex, Cursor, Gemini CLI, goose, Qwen Code and Factory Droid, whose v1 hook commands don't name their event: under the default fail mode, open) or the harness is unknown; a payload nested too deep to read blocks the call. The v1 OpenCode plugin and Antigravity CLI's v1 hooks name their event, so a pre-tool call their hook can't decide is blocked whatever the fail mode. **(1.2)** v2 hooks that `tracekit init` wrote from 1.2 on name their event, so a pre-tool payload that can't be parsed is blocked; a post-tool one is reported on stderr, and the call it ends is completed as an error at the session's next Stop, prompt or end. v2 hooks installed before 1.2 still let an unparseable payload through, unrecorded; `tracekit doctor` (`D-HOOKS-EVENT`) finds event-less commands for Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Gemini CLI, goose, Qwen Code, Factory Droid and Antigravity CLI. | In v1 a Tracekit failure follows the fail mode. A hook that can't parse its payload knows its event only from the command line. Claude Code, Codex, Cursor and Gemini CLI write the payload themselves; OpenCode's plugin passes the model's arguments before OpenCode checks them. Doctor checks those command hooks, goose's, Qwen Code's, Factory Droid's and Antigravity CLI's, but not OpenCode's JavaScript plugin. | Re-run `tracekit init` after upgrading (`tracekit init --dev --v2 --agent NAME` repairs the other coding agents' hooks); in v1 set `TRACEKIT_FAIL_CLOSED=1` or `fail_mode: closed`. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#hooks-name-their-event), [doctor](doctor.md#v2-checks) |
| A v1 dev hook that can't start (its Python moved or was uninstalled, or Tracekit no longer imports) lets the pre-tool call run unrecorded, with an error the coding agent shows, even under the policy's `fail_mode: closed`, unless `TRACEKIT_FAIL_CLOSED=1` is set; on Windows, even then. goose (`on_failure: block`), OpenCode, Antigravity CLI, a Cursor hook init wrote with `failClosed: true` and system mode (unless its config sets `fail_mode: open`) block it. | The hook's command can read an environment variable, but the fail mode in the policy only Tracekit's hook reads; on Windows there is no POSIX shell to wrap the command in. | With `fail_mode: closed`, also set `TRACEKIT_FAIL_CLOSED=1` where the coding agent runs (Claude Code: under `env` in its settings), or use the v2 hooks (`tracekit init --dev --v2`), whose hook that can't start blocks the call. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md) |
| v1 hooks don't hold a Codex patch applied through the shell (`TK-A034` is in the v2 packs); v1 and v2 hooks both decide every file an `apply_patch` call adds, updates, deletes or moves to. | The v1 policy is kept as it was; `TK-A034` is a v2 rule. | Use the v2 hooks (`tracekit init --dev --v2 --agent codex`). | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#on-the-v2-signer) |
| The signer can't tell a real event from a well-formed fake sent by the agent's identity. | Anything that can call the signer as the agent can submit plausible events. | Cross-check with an independent layer: the gateway (`gateway_mandatory`) or, in v1, the model proxy. | [laptop threat model](threat-model-laptop.md#two-properties-kept-apart), [signing](signing.md#what-the-signer-checks-before-signing) |
| Compromised application code can skip the adapter for some calls, and suppress in-process autotrace (L3) too. | Both run in the agent's process. | Run the LLM gateway outside the agent; read `reconcile.*` and `(L3 absent)` in the report. | [server threat model](threat-model-server.md#s2-compromised-application-code) |
| Capture layer L5 (services) is not shipped, and L4 (processes) only as the experimental [kernel sensor import](integrations/tetragon.md) (Linux): a sensor that covered a run throughout makes its processes `observed`, and its effects are the sensor's word, not reconciled. **(1.2)** L6 (network) is the [egress observer](egress.md), hosts only. | Out-of-process capture of services isn't built yet; the coverage matrix says `not covered` for them, and for processes where no sensor covered the run. | On Linux, import a kernel sensor ([Tetragon](integrations/tetragon.md), [Falco](integrations/falco.md)); use the [LLM gateway](gateway.md), the executor and the egress observer as the other out-of-process records. | [server threat model](threat-model-server.md#known-limits) |
| SDK and adapter capture is opt-in per call: only wrapped calls are seen and gated. | An adapter sees only the calls routed through it. | Wrap every tool; each integration lists what it doesn't cover. | [laptop threat model](threat-model-laptop.md#two-properties-kept-apart), [OpenAI Agents](integrations/openai-agents.md#not-covered), [MCP](integrations/mcp.md#not-covered), [Browser Use](integrations/browser-use.md#not-covered), [Claude Agent SDK](integrations/claude-agent-sdk.md#not-covered), [CrewAI](integrations/crewai.md#not-covered), [Pydantic AI](integrations/pydantic-ai.md#not-covered), [Google ADK](integrations/google-adk.md#not-covered), [Haystack](integrations/haystack.md#not-covered), [LlamaIndex](integrations/llamaindex.md#not-covered), [Microsoft Agent Framework](integrations/microsoft-agent-framework.md#not-covered), [Strands](integrations/strands.md#not-covered), [Hermes Agent](integrations/hermes.md#not-covered) |
| Actions inside subprocesses and background processes, and faked command output, aren't observed by the hooks or adapters. | They see the command line; tool results are what the harness reports. | On Linux, import a kernel sensor's records into the run (experimental): the processes, connections and file writes a command started are signed, and `tracekit analyze --v2` flags one no call explains. Elsewhere, run the agent in a sandbox that logs them. | [laptop threat model](threat-model-laptop.md#open-problems) |
| Nothing is recorded after the session's last hook, including transcript edits. | Nothing runs to see it. | Treat the run's end as the last observed point. | [laptop threat model](threat-model-laptop.md#open-problems) |
| **(1.2)** `decided_not_completed` is written at `run.final` only, and doesn't say why the result never came (the agent stopped or crashed, its post-call hook didn't run, or a client dropped it). Calls denied, or asked and never approved, get none: they may not run; a call the executor or a gateway holds gets `held_call_unresolved` instead. A decision is also exempt when a later decision of the same call completed, except one whose approval was consumed and whose call didn't complete with the approved arguments; only the call's latest decision counts as later, so an open decision before a completed one and an open one after it is a gap too. | The signer sees the decide and the missing `complete`, not the agent or the harness's prompt. At most 100 per run are listed, then one gap counts the rest. | Read the gap next to the run's other records and the transcript or app log; a crash usually ends the run early too. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#what-the-signer-records-as-a-gap) |
| **(1.2)** A coding-agent hook completes each call that got no post-tool event (declined in the harness's own permission prompt, interrupted, or a post-tool payload it couldn't read) as an error when the session ends. That result is the hook's word, not the harness's; a session killed before its end event leaves such calls as `decided_not_completed` gaps instead. | No hook event says that a call was declined or interrupted, and a background sub-agent's call can still run when a turn ends; the session's end is the first point where none can. | Read a `tool.result` error "no PostToolUse came" as "not seen to run". | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#what-the-signer-records-as-a-gap) |
| **(1.3)** A coding-agent hook blocks only tool calls. A prompt, Stop, tool result or session event whose hook fails (the signer unreachable, `tracekit` missing or broken) runs unrecorded, with a hook error in the transcript. Hook commands `tracekit init` wrote before 1.3, and Cursor hooks it wrote up to 1.4.2 (`failClosed` on every event), still turn such a failure into a block. | On a prompt, a block erases it; on Stop, it sends the agent back to work in a loop. Every tool call is still decided before it runs. | Read hook errors in the transcript; run `tracekit init` again after upgrading to get the current commands (`tracekit doctor` flags old ones in dev and system mode as `D-HOOKS-EVENT`, and old system-mode ones also as `D-HOOKS-VENV`). | [plugin](../plugin/README.md#when-a-hook-cant-run) |
| **(1.2)** `concurrent_run` covers only runs bound to a harness process (harness binding, Linux system mode): a second run started from inside the same harness process while another session's run is open there, once both have recorded an event since it registered. SDK and server runs, which run several at once by design, and harnesses without binding get no such check. A run started with `parent` (a sub-agent's) gets none either: the agent can read its session's run token, so a second session can pose as a child, and shows only as a `run.child` record in the parent. Which runs overlap is kept in memory and snapshots: a signer restart with no snapshot since forgets a pending one. | One harness process drives one session at a time; without a binding the signer can't tell a forged second run from a legitimate parallel one, and an older session's run left open and idle is no overlap. | Turn on harness binding for coding agents; pass `parent` for a sub-agent's run; read each `run.child` against the harness's transcripts. | [format v2](format-v2.md#3-events) |
| **(1.2)** A killed transcript tailer is a `tailer_lost` gap only in system mode, once it has made no call (heartbeat or record) for 90 s while the run is open, counted from when the hook took its token, and only while the signer runs: the watch is kept in memory. In dev mode the tailer runs as the agent's user, which may not send heartbeats, so a killed tailer leaves no gap. Heartbeats show that a process holding the run's tailer token is alive, not that it reads the session's real transcript: the agent's user starts the tailer (through sudo) and names its transcript, so it can stop the real one and start one of its own on a file it writes. A second tailer token for the run is a `tailer_redelegated` gap, but a decoy handed the first token (the agent's user can read the hook's memory) isn't. A tailer that keeps its heartbeats but stops reading is caught only by its idle limit. | The signer can't watch the tailer's process or see the transcript; the heartbeat is its only signal, and only an identity the agent doesn't control may send it. | Use [system mode](deploy.md#laptop-system-mode) (init as root with `--v2`) where a killed tailer must show; compare the run's transcript records with the transcript itself where it matters; re-check runs that span a signer restart. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#what-the-signer-records-as-a-gap) |
| A session's later run (the signer closed the earlier one after an hour idle, or the session was resumed) records its transcript from where the hook last saw it while the earlier run was open: the lines Claude Code wrote after that run's last hook call (most often the closing text of a turn) can be in both runs. A resumed session is read from where its transcript (Codex's rollout, Gemini CLI's chat recording) ended at SessionStart, so a harness that writes the earlier history only after that hook repeats it in the new run. **(1.5.1)** A Claude Code session that goes on after a SessionEnd with no SessionStart (a message to the session, a usage limit's reset) is read from where its transcripts ended at the new run's first hook: what Claude Code wrote between the SessionEnd and that hook (the new prompt; a first tool call's tool use, then a `reconcile.fabricated` gap) is read by no run, since the earlier run's tailer stopped at the SessionEnd. | The hook can't see how far the earlier run's tailer got; it measures the transcript at its own calls, and a SessionEnd removes the session's state. | Read a session's runs in order (`tracekit view --dev`): each holds what was written while it was open. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#what-was-asked-and-said) |
| **(1.2)** Writes refused because their run is final are counted per run in memory and written about once a minute as a `late_activity` gap in the tenant's signer run; counts since the last write are lost if the signer stops uncleanly. A `close_run` never counts, nor does the first write of each identity and stream (up to 64 per run): a client learns from it that its run ended, after an idle close, and moves to a new run. A run-set lists them with the tenant-level gaps. | The writes can't go into a final run, and the signer-level refusal summary doesn't name the run. | Read `late_activity` in a run-set; if it recurs, check that each client registers a new run once its run ends. | [format v2](format-v2.md#3-events) |
| **(1.2)** Trailing client counter values become a gap only when the client says where its stream ended (`close_run` `last_client_seq`), and only for streams the closing identity wrote (or that never reached the signer); after a signer restart with no snapshot since, a stream written before it can't be ended that way. The LLM gateway says so when it drops a run from memory or stops (SIGTERM or Ctrl-C; a killed gateway says nothing); for a run already final the gap goes in the tenant's signer run, naming the run. A gateway whose identity lacks the `close_run` grant logs the refusal on stderr and leaves no gap. | The signer can't know of values a client never sent, so the gap is the closing identity's word, and its reason names that identity; values up to there that still arrive in the grace period are recorded with no second gap. | Close runs with `last_client_seq` from your own clients; grant gateways `close_run` and stop them with SIGTERM; read a gap's reason for who reported it. | [gateway](gateway.md#what-is-recorded) |
| **(1.2)** A live `reconcile.hook_missing` (written mid-run, for a detector) comes from the LLM gateway's records only; agent-reported L3 is still reconciled at `run.final`. | Agent-reported L3 comes from the process being checked. | Run the gateway for calls that matter. | [gateway](gateway.md#what-is-recorded) |
| Harness binding doesn't stop a process the agent starts inside the harness session, or the agent's user starting its own instance of the registered harness. | Both are legitimately below, or are, the registered program, running as the agent's user. | Run the harness under another user, or use a mandatory gateway. | [FAQ](faq.md#what-does-harness-binding-not-stop) |
| The LLM gateway's record is complete only when the agent can't reach the model around it. | The gateway sees only requests sent to it. | Let only the gateway reach the provider (network policy) and set `gateway_mandatory: true`. | [gateway](gateway.md#calling-it) |
| A tool the policy's `structured_output:` names gets no L3 cross-check: a decide for it needs no tool use in the model's response, and a tool use of it needs no decide, even under `gateway_mandatory`, so any tool of that name runs with no decide unseen. The browser pack names Browser Use's actions (`browser_use:*`) only; its output tool with Anthropic models (`AgentOutput`) is the deployment's to name. A child policy can't remove a name its parent declares. | Browser Use asks the model for its actions as structured output, which carries no tool use id to match a decide with. | Read Browser Use's records as decided by the signer, not as proven asked for by the model; declare only names whose calls really come as structured output and that no other tool of yours uses. | [gateway](gateway.md#reconciliation), [Browser Use](integrations/browser-use.md#the-browser-policy-pack) |
| **(1.2)** Tools the provider runs itself (web search, code execution, hosted MCP, ...) can't be held for a decision: they run mid-response. The gateway records their input where the provider returns it (not for image generation) and can only refuse a whole request that declares one (`provider_tools`). OpenAI `shell` and `tool_search` tools count as provider-run, since the provider may host them: with `allow`, list them if your client runs them. | No client-side step happens between the model asking and the provider running them, and a request doesn't always say who runs those two. | Deny provider tools you can't accept in gateway.yaml `provider_tools` (`mcp` covers every hosted MCP server, `mcp:<label>` one); the client gets a 403 naming them. | [gateway](gateway.md#provider-run-tools-12) |
| **(1.3)** Verified tool results catch an edited tool result for a call the signer already completed, and record a result sent before its call completed. They don't catch text the agent adds outside tool results; a tool call the agent writes into the history itself with its result (reconciliation flags it at run.final, `result_without_call` or, under `gateway_mandatory`, `fabricated`, unless the agent also forges a decide, a result and a model report with `gateway_mandatory` off); results past a request's newest 512 or past the 1 MiB of them the gateway digests (newest first), which the agent chooses by adding results of its own: each such call is a signed `capture.gap{result_unchecked}`, and past the newest 1024 not even that; a request under the token of a run the agent registered itself; a result OpenAI Responses doesn't resend (`previous_response_id`) after its first send; and, with the signer unreachable and `model` failing open, a request the gateway forwards unchecked. | The check compares what the gateway sees against the signed results of the same run, by tool_call_id: content outside a tool result, a call with no result in that run, and a request never sent or never checked have nothing to compare with. | Set `gateway_mandatory` and let only the gateway reach the provider; keep the `model` class failing closed; read a run's `result_without_call` and `fabricated` records with its mismatches. | [gateway](gateway.md#verified-tool-results) |
| **(1.3)** Verified tool results are off unless signer.yaml sets `verify_tool_results`. A client whose recorded result isn't the value the model is sent mismatches on honest runs, and `refuse` then stops the agent with a 403: every result of the Claude Code hooks (a digest of the raw output) and the Claude Agent SDK integration (the structured tool response); in Pydantic AI a tool returning a list, which Anthropic gets as one text block per item; in Google ADK under `AnthropicLlm` every result but a dict (and a dict with a `content` key or only `result`), under `LiteLlm` a model or dataclass return (ADK sends its Python repr); and a result of several content blocks (Agent Framework, Strands). Gemini requests (ADK's default) don't pass through the gateway, so nothing is checked. Calls decided under ids of the client's own (the MCP integration, the MCP gateway, LlamaIndex's `ReActAgent` and `CodeActAgent`) are unchecked, and so are calls completed with no result (a Pydantic AI output function's: the model reads `Final result processed.`), error results and results whose content redaction changed. A match says the output was passed on unchanged, not that it is true. | The signer compares commitments: equal values, or a value and its digest, match; two renderings of the same output don't, and a commitment over redacted or absent content opens nothing to compare. | Run `record` first; set `refuse` only once `tracekit verify` shows no `result_mismatch` on honest runs of your own agent, model and tools. | [gateway](gateway.md#verified-tool-results) |
| **(1.3)** Verified tool results: `refuse` refuses a request only for an edited result of a completed call. A result sent before its call completed is a signed `result_mismatch` and the request is forwarded, so an agent can send a result of its own for an allowed call it never completes. A request's results past 1 MiB (as JSON, newest first; a result the run's previous request also sent counts for nothing) are not compared, and neither is any one result over 1 MiB: each such call is a signed `capture.gap{result_unchecked}` (a `gaps` warning in `tracekit verify`), never refused. | A complete can be lost on an honest run (the client gives up after its retries) and every later request sends the result again: refusing would stop the run for good. The gateway digests results in pure Python before forwarding (15 MB of numbers took 3.4 s); the cap bounds that work per request, and a gateway that restarted has no digests cached, so refusing unchecked results would stop honest long runs. | Read a `result_mismatch` whose detail says the result was sent before the call's was recorded with that call's `decided_not_completed` on `run.final`, and a `result_unchecked` gap as a result nothing compared; keep the tool results a model reads under 1 MiB. | [gateway](gateway.md#verified-tool-results) |
| With `durability: ack-on-write` (the default), a power loss can drop records already acknowledged. | Acknowledging before fsync is faster. | Set `durability: ack-on-fsync` in production. | [doctor](doctor.md#v2-checks) |
| A data directory on NFS, SMB, FUSE or similar can't be trusted for locking and fsync. | Those file systems don't give the guarantees the store needs. | Put `data_dir` on a local disk (`D-DATA-FS`). | [doctor](doctor.md#v2-checks) |
| Gemini CLI tool calls carry no call id; Tracekit pairs them by tool and arguments, first in first out. | The harness doesn't send one. | None needed; be aware when reading paired results. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md) |
| Reasoning capture reads only Claude Code's transcript, and the v1 model proxy fronts only the Anthropic API. | Other harnesses use other formats and APIs. | Use the v2 gateway or OpenTelemetry for model-side cross-checks. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md) |
| Tracekit doesn't see whether Claude Code's sandbox started for a command, what it blocked inside one beyond what Claude Code writes into the result, or the sandbox settings (they aren't signed into the run). | The sandbox runs inside Claude Code's process and keeps no record; hooks see the call and its result. | Set `sandbox.failIfUnavailable: true` so Claude Code never falls back to running unsandboxed; `tracekit doctor` warns when it isn't set (`D-CLAUDE-SANDBOX`). | [Claude Code's sandbox](integrations/claude-code-sandbox.md) |
| Tracekit doesn't see whether Codex ran a command in its sandbox: a request to run outside it (`sandbox_permissions: "require_escalated"`), Codex's answer, or a command an execpolicy `allow` rule let run outside it. Codex's sandbox and approval settings aren't signed into the run, and no rule holds an escalation as `TK-A003` holds Claude Code's unsandboxed retry. | Codex's `PreToolUse` hook sends a shell call's command only; the sandbox and its approvals run inside Codex. | Keep escalations with a person (`approval_policy = "on-request"`, `approvals_reviewer = "user"`), or set `approval_policy = "never"` so Codex refuses them; keep `allow` rules narrow. The policy still decides every command before Codex's approval. | [Codex's sandbox](integrations/codex-sandbox.md) |
| A process's `CODEX_TOOL_CALL_ID` names the Codex call that started it, the `tool_call_id` of that call's records, but the process and its children can change or remove it. | Codex sets it as a label for correlation, not authorization. | Use it to find the signed call behind an effect; read a missing or unknown id as unexplained, and a match as a pointer, not proof. | [Codex's sandbox](integrations/codex-sandbox.md#codex_tool_call_id) |
| Gemini CLI's whole-CLI sandbox (`tools.sandbox`, `GEMINI_SANDBOX`, `-s`) runs without Tracekit: in a container the hooks `tracekit init` writes don't run and the session isn't recorded, with no gap; under Seatbelt the hook can't keep its state and blocks every call. | Gemini runs the whole CLI, its hooks included, inside that sandbox: a container gets your user settings without their hooks, and Seatbelt lets a process write only the workspace, temporary and cache directories. | Use Gemini's tool sandbox (`security.toolSandboxing: true`) instead. `tracekit doctor` warns when Gemini's settings turn the whole-CLI sandbox on (`D-GEMINI-SANDBOX`), but can't see `GEMINI_SANDBOX` or `-s` at launch. | [Gemini CLI's sandbox](integrations/gemini-sandbox.md#not-the-whole-cli-sandbox) |
| Tracekit doesn't see sandbox permissions Gemini CLI grants outside a call's arguments (what its approval mode allows, an expansion Gemini proposes itself, one allowed for good), and Gemini's sandbox settings aren't signed into the run. | Gemini's `BeforeTool` hook sends the call's arguments; the sandbox and its approvals run inside Gemini. | Avoid YOLO mode and answer expansion requests once, not for good; `TK-A004` holds each expansion a call's `additional_permissions` asks for. | [Gemini CLI's sandbox](integrations/gemini-sandbox.md#what-tracekit-sees-and-doesnt) |
| `tracekit doctor` doesn't see Gemini CLI's folder trust: Gemini ignores an untrusted project's settings, so `D-GEMINI-SANDBOX` can read a project setting Gemini doesn't apply, or miss what Gemini does. | Folder trust is Gemini's own state, kept outside the settings files doctor reads. | Put the sandbox settings that matter in your user settings (`~/.gemini/settings.json`), which folder trust doesn't switch off. | [Gemini CLI's sandbox](integrations/gemini-sandbox.md#what-tracekit-sees-and-doesnt) |
| Qwen Code runs a project's hooks only in a trusted folder: in an untrusted one the hooks `tracekit init --project` wrote don't run, so nothing gates or records that session's calls. A `disableAllHooks: true` in any Qwen Code settings file turns every hook off, Tracekit's included, and so do `--safe-mode` and `--bare` at launch. With `QWEN_HOME` set, Qwen Code reads its user settings from that directory, not from `~/.qwen/settings.json`, where `tracekit init --agent qwen` writes. | Qwen Code skips an untrusted workspace's hooks and honours its own switches without telling them; `tracekit doctor` reads neither its folder trust nor its settings layers. | Install in the user's settings (`tracekit init --dev --agent qwen`), trust the folder, leave `disableAllHooks` unset, don't launch with `--safe-mode` or `--bare`, unset `QWEN_HOME` (or copy the hooks into `$QWEN_HOME/settings.json`), and check with `tracekit view --dev` that calls are recorded. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md) |
| Qwen Code's model text and reasoning are not recorded: its transcript is hashed at each hook when the payload names it, but not read, so `tracekit analyze --v2` has only the hook's prompt and no reasoning for its runs. | Its transcript is not in a format Tracekit parses, and its hooks carry no model text. | Route model calls through the LLM gateway for text the agent can't write; read Qwen Code runs by their calls. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#what-was-asked-and-said) |
| The Claude Code hook records as typed every part of a prompt that isn't in a wrapper it recognises (such as a paste block, a skill's body, a message from another session, agent, teammate, plugin, observer or channel, a task or system notification, a scheduled task, a message Claude Code marks as from a non-user source, uploads, command output, system reminders and fetched web content). Wrapper text it doesn't recognise therefore counts as the person's: a value it holds isn't untrusted (a `from: untrusted` rule allows a call that uses it), its constraints are live, and `tracekit analyze --v2` reads it as a request. Claude Code 2.1.286 sends three kinds of turn no person typed to the hook with no wrapper at all, so they count as typed too: a prompt a plugin submits, a notice from another Claude Code session, and a poll event (a custom element such as `<ci-status>`). Once Claude Code sends its payload's `source` field, a turn whose source isn't `user` or `sdk` is recorded whole as untrusted (`claude-code:<source>`), and none of its constraints are live. The Gemini CLI hook recognises only its referenced files block; the Codex, Cursor, Qwen Code and Factory Droid hooks recognise no wrapper. | A hook gets the prompt as one string, with no signed boundary between what was typed and what the harness added; the wrapper spellings are the ones seen in each harness, and a harness release can add one. Claude Code adds its wrappers to those three kinds of turn only after the hooks run; its hook input schema defines `source` (`user`, `sdk`, `system`, `loop_wakeup`, `schedule_wakeup`, `poll_event`), but 2.1.286 leaves it out. | Report wrapper text Tracekit records as typed. Where a value must not be used, put a deny or ask rule on the destination, not only `from: untrusted`. Install only plugins and poll sources you trust to speak for you, and update Claude Code: a release that sends `source` closes the three bare paths with no change on your side. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#what-was-asked-and-said) |
| A paste Claude Code sends inline (its default) is recorded as typed, and its constraints are live. A recognised wrapper inside it, or typed text that looks like one, makes the rest of the prompt untrusted from there, text typed after it included: a value in it isn't trusted, and `tracekit analyze --v2` reads none of it as a request. When the wrapper starts a line, constraints after it aren't live, and Claude Code shows a notice naming them; when it follows other text on its line (a tag's name in a sentence), they are live up to the next wrapper that starts a line, and analyze checks them. Constraints in a wrapped part that starts a line (a paste block, a slash command's arguments, a scheduled prompt, a relayed message) are recorded but not live. | The hook can't tell a typed tag from one the harness added, or a pasted line from a typed one; wrapped parts are text the harness added or relayed, which the person didn't type. Claude Code starts a line with each wrapper it adds, so one mid-line may be typed, and a constraint only holds more calls. | Type constraints before a large paste or in a prompt of their own; restating one after a wrapper at the start of a line doesn't make it live. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#what-was-asked-and-said) |
| Untrusted text in one prompt is recorded up to 50,134,272 characters, shared equally across the wrapper kinds in it. Past that, the text isn't recorded: the hook leaves one event number unsent before a `claude-code:harness-cut` note naming the cut kinds, so the signer writes a signed `client_counter_gap` and marks the run's provenance index partial, and Claude Code shows a notice. For the rest of the run, every `from: untrusted` rule applies to each call its pattern matches, whatever the value's source. | Each observation the hook sends the signer is one frame of at most 1 MiB. The cap is 48 frames' worth of characters, so the hook doesn't hold an ASCII prompt long (about 7 s at the cap); text that isn't ASCII takes more frames, up to about four times as many, since each frame holds 1 MiB of UTF-8. A cut value never reaches the signer's untrusted-value index, so the gap marks the index incomplete rather than let such a value pass. | Keep pasted and attached text in one prompt under the cap. After a cut, expect `from: untrusted` rules to ask about (or deny) every call they match until the run ends; start a new session to return to normal. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#what-was-asked-and-said) |
| Qwen Code records as the person's prompt only a `UserPromptSubmit` that carries `submitted_prompt` (Qwen Code 0.21.1 on in its TUI, 0.21.12 on for `qwen -p`), and records that text, not `prompt`, into which Qwen Code expands `@file` contents and a large paste (over 1,000 characters or 10 lines: `submitted_prompt` holds its `[Pasted Content N chars]` placeholder). The rest are not recorded: its continuations after tool results (an empty `prompt`), a Stop hook's reason it sends back to the model, and typed prompts Qwen Code sends without the field: Qwen Code before those releases; the TUI with Vim mode on (or input it can't trace to the composer); ACP clients and daemon hosts that send no `qwen.submittedPrompt` declaration (Web Shell sends one; realtime voice handoffs and channel messages, a person's included, never carry it). The live constraints the hook hands the signer are read from the same text, so a Stop hook's reason, a referenced file's contents or a large paste's text never state one. | Qwen Code fires `UserPromptSubmit` for those sends too, and `submitted_prompt` is the only field that tells a submission from them. | Use Qwen Code 0.21.1 or later (0.21.12 for `qwen -p`) with Vim mode off, and an ACP client that declares `qwen.submittedPrompt`; type a constraint you want held into the prompt itself, not into a large paste or a referenced file; read a prompt it didn't record from Qwen Code's own session history. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#what-was-asked-and-said) |
| Qwen Code on native Windows is untested. It runs hooks under cmd.exe, so `tracekit init --agent qwen` writes the hook command in cmd.exe's form, the Python path in quotes when it needs them and bare otherwise; an earlier init wrote PowerShell's `& "..."` form when the path had a space, which cmd.exe can't run. A hook that can't start (its Python moved or was uninstalled) or crashes isn't wrapped to fail closed: Qwen Code takes that as a hook error that doesn't block, and the call runs unchecked and unrecorded. | Windows has no POSIX shell to wrap the hook in. | Run `tracekit init --dev --v2 --agent qwen` again after upgrading Tracekit and after moving or reinstalling Python, or use WSL; check with `tracekit view --dev` that calls are recorded. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md) |
| `tracekit srt-decider` sees only requests to the hosts in its `--allow-domain` list; srt-proxy refuses the rest itself and Tracekit records nothing of them. | srt-proxy's decider protocol has the decider name its hosts before the first request, and no entry matches every host. | List every host the workload needs; a missing one shows in the workload as `403` with `X-Deny-Reason: host_not_allowed`. | [sandbox-runtime](integrations/sandbox-runtime.md#setup) |
| A request `tracekit srt-decider` allowed is recorded as allowed, not as sent: Tracekit doesn't see the response, nor whether srt-proxy forwarded it (it still refuses a host that resolves to a private address, and an answer that comes after its 5 s wait). | The protocol carries requests and verdicts, nothing after. | Keep the signer close to the decider so answers come in time; route calls whose result matters through the [tool executor](executor.md), which records it. | [sandbox-runtime](integrations/sandbox-runtime.md#what-tracekit-sees-and-doesnt) |
| An `ask` through `tracekit srt-decider` refuses the request at once (`403`); the request is allowed only when the workload sends it again after a person approved it. | srt-proxy waits 5 s for each answer. | Have the workload retry a `403` that names an approval, or send the request again yourself once it is approved. | [sandbox-runtime](integrations/sandbox-runtime.md#what-is-decided) |
| A `tracekit srt-decider` approval binds the method, URL and body digest: not the headers, nor the body of a GET, HEAD or OPTIONS. A body over 32 MiB is refused. | srt-proxy shows a body only when asked, up to 32 MiB, and not for those methods. | Where a header or such a body changes what a request does, deny that host by policy rather than ask. | [sandbox-runtime](integrations/sandbox-runtime.md#what-is-decided) |
| One `tracekit srt-decider` is one run, whatever process sent a request, and the approvals it waits on live in its memory: after a restart the same request asks again. When the signer closes the run (no request for `idle_s`, an hour by default), the decider refuses the next request and stops srt-proxy. | srt-proxy's requests name no client; a closed run records nothing more. | Run one srt-proxy and decider per agent run, under a supervisor that restarts it; after a restart, approve the new request. | [sandbox-runtime](integrations/sandbox-runtime.md#what-tracekit-sees-and-doesnt) |
| `tracekit srt-decider` decides each request on its method, URL and the body's SHA-256 only: content rules never see the body, so a rule on what a request sends (a secret, a customer record) can't match it. | Only the digest goes to the signer, so the run never holds the body; the digest binds an approval to it. | Decide such hosts by host and method; send calls whose content matters through the [tool executor](executor.md) or the [MCP gateway](mcp-gateway.md), whose policy sees their arguments. | [sandbox-runtime](integrations/sandbox-runtime.md#what-is-decided) |
| **(1.2)** A run is recorded as a child (`register_run` `parent`) only when the caller presents the parent's run token as the identity it was issued to, on the parent's signer, while the parent is not final. | The token proves the caller may act for the parent run; a final run takes nothing more. | Start sub-agent runs from the code holding the parent's handle (`client.run(..., parent=handle)`), or with a `delegate_run` token for the sub-agent's identity, before the parent finishes. The refusal (`run_token_invalid`, `unknown_run`, `run_closed`) says which. | [format v2](format-v2.md#3-events) |
| v1 OTLP receiver and ingest gateway are experimental. | They are being rebuilt; they run only with `--experimental`. | Use the v2 signer's OTLP import. | [OpenTelemetry](otel.md#receiving-traces) |
| v1 OTLP policy is evaluated after the fact: a call a rule would deny is recorded as `flag`, not stopped. | Spans arrive when the work is done. | Block with hooks, an SDK `tool()` or an adapter. | [OpenTelemetry](otel.md#what-it-means) |
| **(1.2)** An MCP call decided as `mcp:<server>/<tool>` reconciles with the model's tool use only when the model's name is the bare `<tool>`, `mcp__<server>__<tool>` (Claude Code) or `mcp_<server>__<tool>` (OpenAI Agents JS with `includeServerInToolNames`); its arguments are compared under that name. A framework that shows the server another way (mcp-agent's `<server>_<tool>`) gets `args_mismatch` for each call; with a bare name, two servers' same-named tools in one model turn are told apart only by call id. | Only names that can't be a different tool's are accepted: a suffix match would let an agent rename `delete_account` to `mcp:x/account`. | Expect `args_mismatch` for such frameworks until the policy can list their servers; keep the model's call ids (the shipped adapters do). | [MCP](integrations/mcp.md) |
| **(1.2)** With a bare model name (OpenAI Agents JS, the Vercel AI SDK, the mcp Python client), nothing ties the server in `mcp:<server>/<tool>` to the model's call: an agent that decides the model's `query` as `mcp:notes/query` instead of `mcp:postgres/query` is reconciled, and rules keyed on the server (`mcp:postgres/*`) don't apply. A server or tool with an edge `_` or a `__`, or a tool named like a prefixed form, gets no alias at all. | The model never saw the server; only the agent's adapter names it. | Key MCP rules on the tool name (it is also checked under its bare name) or use names that carry the server (`includeServerInToolNames`, Claude Code); put servers that need server-level rules behind the MCP gateway, which decides what it forwards. | [MCP](integrations/mcp.md), [MCP gateway](mcp-gateway.md) |
| **(1.2)** A long-running signer keeps a small stub of every run it has seen (about 1 KB in memory, 0.5 KB in each snapshot, plus the file store's per-record index), each final run's first late writers (up to 64), and every incident, resolved ones too (`incident_list` serves them); the request-id cache holds at most 100,000 answers (10,000 per identity). Snapshots are written whole on the writer, pausing writes for about 1 s per 300,000 runs. | Final runs answer tokens, late writes and `run_exists` from memory; the stubs are the signer's index of what it has signed. | Restart into a new store (a new `data_dir`, the old one kept for export and verification) once a store holds millions of runs; size memory at about 3 KB per run. | [deployment](deploy.md) |
| OpenCode (1.x) sends plugins no session or prompt events, and no `tool.execute.after` for a call that fails, is declined in its own permission prompt or is aborted: its run closes only at the signer's idle timeout, no prompts, live constraints or model text are recorded, and those calls stay `decided_not_completed` gaps. | Its plugin API has tool hooks only, and runs the after hook only for a call that completed. | Read such gaps as calls that didn't complete; use Claude Code, Codex or Gemini CLI where prompts and session ends must be in the record. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md) |
| **(experimental)** goose support hasn't been checked in a live goose session (1.48.0 or later). On Windows goose runs hooks with `sh -c`, so without `sh` on PATH (Git Bash) every call is blocked. Code Mode (`execute_javascript`, and `execute_typescript` or `execute_bash` before goose 1.56) is denied, so Code Mode can't be used with Tracekit. | The tests drive goose's documented payloads and its source's hook runner; Code Mode runs tool calls through `dispatch_tool`, which runs no hooks. | Use goose's own tools rather than Code Mode; on Windows install Git Bash. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md) |
| goose sends no tool result on PostToolUse and no error text on PostToolUseFailure: a completed call's tool.result carries no output, and a failed call's carries ok=False with no error text. Its installed hooks are tool events only (no session or prompt events), so no prompts, live constraints, transcript hashes or model text are recorded. | goose's hook payload table lists no result or error field for tool events, and Tracekit installs only PreToolUse, PostToolUse and PostToolUseFailure. | Read a goose run's calls from their decisions and arguments, not from outputs; use Claude Code, Codex or Gemini CLI where results and prompts must be in the record. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md) |
| OpenCode's experimental `batch` tool (`experimental.batch_tool`, OpenCode 1.x before 1.4 only) is blocked whatever its calls: the plugin refuses it before it runs. | OpenCode runs the calls inside a batch without plugin hooks, so Tracekit could neither decide nor record them. | Leave `experimental.batch_tool` off; the model then makes each call itself, and each is decided. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md) |
| While Tracekit decides a call or a person answers a held one, the OpenCode plugin waits synchronously, so OpenCode doesn't stream or take other input until the answer; OpenCode 2.0 doesn't load 1.x plugins, so its hooks would stop with no error. | Bun's synchronous child process keeps the before hook simple and fail-closed. | Answer holds promptly (`tracekit approvals list`); pin OpenCode 1.x until Tracekit supports 2.0. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md) |
| Factory Droid's documented hook stdin gives a tool call no id. Two identical calls in flight are paired first in first out, so their results cannot be distinguished if they complete in another order. An allowed call with no PostToolUse (declined in Droid's permission prompt, blocked by another hook, or failed; Droid has no PostToolUseFailure) stays queued, so the next identical call's result can be paired to it and the call that ran closes as an error at session end. | Only the tool name and arguments connect Droid's PreToolUse and PostToolUse events. | Avoid concurrent identical calls when their result attribution matters. Blocked calls leave the queue so a later allowed call does not inherit their id. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md) |
| **(experimental)** Factory Droid support has not been checked in a live authenticated session or on native Windows. Its transcript format is not read, so model text and reasoning are not captured. Its documented events include no PostToolUseFailure; the minimum version is unknown, and whether Task-started sub-droids fire hooks is unknown. ApplyPatch's field and format are from Factory CLI 0.249.0's tool listing, not a captured hook payload. | Tests drive the documented stdin through Tracekit's real v1 pipeline and the v2 signer; the tool listing does not exercise hooks. | Verify a denied and a held call in Droid before relying on the integration; inspect the run with `tracekit last`. A failed call without PostToolUse remains incomplete until SessionEnd or the signer's gap handling. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md) |
| An agent's edit of Factory Droid's settings or hooks (`.factory/`, `~/.factory/`) is flagged (`TK-F009`, `TK-F015`), not held, as for the other harnesses. Managed-only hooks or globally disabled hooks can also keep the user and project hooks from running. | The settings rules flag the whole folder, which agents also touch in normal work; Droid applies its own settings before launching hooks. | Keep hook configuration outside the agent's write access, leave hooks enabled, check the effective hooks in Droid's `/hooks` UI, and verify calls appear in `tracekit last`. | [Droid hooks](https://docs.factory.com/harness/hooks) |
| Factory Droid's `UserPromptSubmit` prompt is recorded as the person's (trusted), and the hook reads live constraints from it, but it isn't confirmed that only a prompt the person typed fires it. A `Task`-started sub-droid's prompt or a Stop hook's continuation may fire it too (Qwen Code fires it with a Stop hook's reason), and would then be recorded as the person's. | Droid's hooks reference doesn't say what else fires `UserPromptSubmit`, and Tracekit hasn't checked it in a live Droid session. | Until a live session confirms it, read a Droid prompt in `tracekit view --dev` as what Droid handed over, not proof that the person typed it, and check it against what you typed; put what the agent must not do in the policy, not only in the prompt. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md) |
| **(experimental)** Antigravity CLI (agy) support has not been checked in a live agy session or on native Windows. Tests drive agy's documented hook payloads through Tracekit's v1 pipeline and the v2 signer. That agy sends the same `stepIdx` and `toolCall` with a call's `PreToolUse` and `PostToolUse`, which pairs them, is its documented contract, not yet seen. agy 1.2.1 to 1.2.3 ran no hooks on some machines ([#1008](https://github.com/google-antigravity/antigravity-cli/issues/1008), working again in 1.3.0); before 1.2.17 a project's `.agents/hooks.json` is read only for a folder passed with `--add-dir` ([#1052](https://github.com/google-antigravity/antigravity-cli/issues/1052)). | Running agy needs a Google sign-in, which the tests don't have. The skipped hooks are agy's bugs. | Use agy 1.3.0 or later. Before relying on it, check in agy that a call the policy denies is blocked and a held call waits for `tracekit approvals approve`, and that `tracekit view --dev` shows each call with its outcome. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#antigravity-cli) |
| On Windows, an Antigravity CLI hook in dev mode that can't start (its Python moved or was uninstalled) isn't wrapped to answer agy's JSON deny: agy takes it as a failed hook, and the call runs unchecked and unrecorded. Before agy 1.4.7, a post-tool hook that failed also replaced the call's result with an error. | Windows has no POSIX shell to wrap the hook in; elsewhere a wrapper answers at exit 0, a deny before the call and `{}` after it. | After moving or reinstalling Python, run `tracekit init --dev --v2 --agent antigravity` again, or use WSL; check with `tracekit view --dev` that calls are recorded. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#antigravity-cli) |
| Antigravity CLI's prompts, live constraints, model text and tool output are not recorded. Tracekit installs only agy's `PreToolUse` and `PostToolUse` hooks, and agy's `PostToolUse` sends a failed call's error text but no output. With no session events, an agy run closes at the signer's idle timeout. | Tracekit hooks agy's tool calls only and doesn't read agy's transcript. | Put what the agent must not do in the policy, not only in the prompt; route model calls through the LLM gateway for text the agent can't write; read agy runs by their calls. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#antigravity-cli) |
| Antigravity CLI loads its hooks and never runs them when Tracekit's hook is `"enabled": false` (in `~/.gemini/config/hooks.json` or a workspace's `.agents/hooks.json`), or when agy signs in with a Gemini API key (antigravity-cli#893) or Application Default Credentials (antigravity-cli#1310). Then no agy call is decided or recorded, there is no gap, and agy doesn't say so. | agy skips the hooks itself, and Tracekit sees nothing of a session whose hooks never run. | Sign agy in with a Google account, and don't set `"enabled": false` on Tracekit's hook in any `hooks.json`. Run `tracekit doctor` from agy's shell: `D-AGY-HOOKS-RUN` warns for each of these it can see and says what to change. | [doctor](doctor.md#v2-checks) |
| The global `~/.gemini/config/hooks.json` that `tracekit init --agent antigravity` writes is read by the Antigravity IDE app too ([#1329](https://github.com/google-antigravity/antigravity-cli/issues/1329)): the app's tool calls are decided and recorded as well, as agent `antigravity`, and a held call stalls the app's turn until someone answers it. #1329 reports the app running a call a hook answered `allow` that headless `agy -p` still declines ([#1053](https://github.com/google-antigravity/antigravity-cli/issues/1053)); whether that skips the app's own approval prompt is untested. | agy, the IDE and the app share one global hook config, and a hook payload doesn't say which of them sent it. | Install per workspace (`--project`: `.agents/hooks.json`) to keep the hooks out of other workspaces; answer held calls from another terminal (`tracekit approvals list`); keep the app's own approval settings as they are. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#antigravity-cli) |
| An Antigravity CLI call Tracekit allowed that gets no matching `PostToolUse` (declined by agy's own permissions: headless `agy -p` declines every command without a grant, [#1053](https://github.com/google-antigravity/antigravity-cli/issues/1053)) stays a `decided_not_completed` gap. `invoke_subagent` is decided as `Task`, but agy creates the sub-agent even when the hook denies it ([#640](https://github.com/google-antigravity/antigravity-cli/issues/640)). | With no session events, nothing tells the hook that agy declined a call; agy doesn't honour `deny` for sub-agent tools. | Read such a gap on an `antigravity` run as a call agy may have declined; don't count on a deny or a hold to stop agy's sub-agents. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#antigravity-cli) |
| Tracekit reads Antigravity CLI's `BypassSandbox` (a `run_command` run outside agy's terminal sandbox) from the call's `PreToolUse` input, where agy 1.4.4 sends it although its hooks reference doesn't list it ([#1304](https://github.com/google-antigravity/antigravity-cli/issues/1304)), and decides it as Claude Code's `dangerouslyDisableSandbox`: `TK-A003` holds the call, and its reason names Claude Code's sandbox. A release that renames or drops the field runs such commands outside the sandbox with no hold. | The field is undocumented (#1304 asks for it to be); `TK-A003` already holds the same request from Claude Code. | Keep agy's own approval for commands outside its sandbox on; read a `TK-A003` hold on an `antigravity` run as agy's sandbox. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#antigravity-cli) |
| While Tracekit holds an Antigravity CLI call for approval, agy waits (up to the hook's 600 s timeout) and may show nothing: the approval id the hook prints goes to stderr, which agy isn't known to display. An agent's edit of agy's hook files (`~/.gemini/config/hooks.json`, a project's `.agents/hooks.json`) is flagged (`TK-F009`, `TK-F015`), not held, and whether a project hook named `tracekit` replaces the global one is untested. | agy acts on a hook's answer only once it returns, and logs no line when a hook runs ([#893](https://github.com/google-antigravity/antigravity-cli/issues/893)); the settings rules flag rather than hold, as for the other harnesses. | List and answer held calls from another terminal (`tracekit approvals list`); check that agy's `/hooks` lists `tracekit`; keep `.agents/` out of the agent's write access. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#antigravity-cli) |

## Policy

| Limit | Why it is so | What to do | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| An agent's edit of its own harness's settings or hooks (`.claude/settings.json`, `~/.gemini/settings.json`, `.codex/`, `~/.cursor/hooks.json`, OpenCode's `.opencode/`, `opencode.json` and, from the shell, `~/.config/opencode` (a file tool's write there is denied, `TK-D009`), Qwen Code's `.qwen/` and `~/.qwen/settings.json`, Factory Droid's `.factory/`, Antigravity CLI's `~/.gemini/config/hooks.json` and `.agents/hooks.json`, goose's `.agents/plugins/` and `~/.config/goose`, by a file-editing tool (Write, Edit or MultiEdit) or a recognized shell write) is flagged (`TK-F009`, `TK-F015`), not held, and once it turns the sandbox off, `TK-A003`, `TK-A004` and `TK-A014` have nothing to hold: later commands run outside it with no escape-hatch argument. The file rule reads the normalized path, and the shell rule reads `WRITE_TARGET:` and `DELETE_TARGET:` effects (redirects, `tee`, `cp`, `mv`, `sed -i`, `rm`, `find -delete`, `git rm`, awk's `print > "file"`, …). Both also match casefolded paths. Reads and content that merely names these paths do not match. `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` keeps its argv/raw-argument check in `TK-F016`. Under `QWEN_HOME`, the shell rule matches only the variable's name (`$QWEN_HOME/settings.json`); a file tool's write to the directory it names is not matched. A write through any other variable, a script or another program (`curl -o`, `wget -O`, `touch`, `rsync`, `git restore`, an editor, an interpreter one-liner) is not matched. | The rules cover every file under those directories (skills, commands, memory), which agents edit in normal work; holding all of them would ask about most sessions. | Read `TK-F009` and `TK-F015` flags as possible sandbox changes; keep the sandbox's settings where the agent can't write (managed settings, a read-only mount); add an `ask` rule of your own for the settings files your agents never need to touch. | [Claude Code's sandbox](integrations/claude-code-sandbox.md#what-tracekit-sees-and-doesnt), [Gemini CLI's sandbox](integrations/gemini-sandbox.md) |
| `TK-A003` holds a call only when it carries `dangerouslyDisableSandbox: true`. Commands matching `excludedCommands`, hooks and MCP servers run outside the sandbox without it. | Claude Code marks only the unsandboxed retry; the rest are outside the sandbox by configuration. | Keep `excludedCommands` short; the policy still decides each excluded command and MCP tool call. | [Claude Code's sandbox](integrations/claude-code-sandbox.md#what-tracekit-sees-and-doesnt) |
| `TK-A014` holds a call whose `allowed_domains` names a host; it doesn't see hosts opened by the sandbox's own settings (`network.allowedDomains`), and it can't tell which of the listed hosts a command then reached. | The hook sees the call's arguments, not the sandbox's settings or its proxy's traffic. | Keep the hosts a project always needs in `network.allowedDomains` (a person edits the settings) and approve the rest one command at a time. | [Claude Code's sandbox](integrations/claude-code-sandbox.md#per-command-allowed-domains-tk-a014) |
| The `decide` RPC answers `allow` for a flagged call; only the signed `policy.decision` says `flag`. | `decision` tells the client what to do, and clients since RPC 15 know only `allow`, `deny` and `ask`; the signer can't tell a client's version. | Read a flag from the record, or from the flag rule's id in the response's `rule_ids`. | [policy](policy-v2.md#decisions) |
| Policy rules are tripwires that make attempts visible, not the protection itself. | They match strings; a rewording a rule doesn't anticipate isn't matched. The protection is the signer's isolation, its storage and the witnesses. | Rely on isolation and witnesses; read E3's misses. | [policy](policy-v2.md#packs-shipped), [evaluation](evaluation.md#e3-policy-gate) |
| Shell variables are not expanded and encodings are not decoded. | Static parsing can't know runtime values. | Opaque commands ask (`TK-SHELL-PARSE`); keep them held. | [policy](policy-v2.md#what-a-rule-matches-the-subject) |
| In a `$(( ))` whose text has `$(` or a backtick, the parser reads each of those command substitutions, which get the rules as any command does (`echo $(( $(nproc) * 2 ))` is read as `nproc`). It asks (`TK-SHELL-PARSE`) instead when that text also has a quote (`'`, `"` or `$'`), a newline, a `[` (an array index), parentheses that don't pair up, or a backtick or backslash together with a parenthesis. | bash reads quotes, brackets and parentheses inside `$(( ))` its own way, so the parser can't be sure where such text starts and ends. | Approve the held call, or compute the value in a command of its own first (`m=$(sysctl -n hw.memsize); echo $(( m / 1073741824 ))`). | [policy](policy-v2.md#what-a-rule-matches-the-subject) |
